\title[Alternating-map functors and vector bundles]{Analyticity of alternating-map functors\\ and vector bundles}

\begin{document}
\begin{abstract}
A $C^n$ functor on normed spaces acts on $C^n$ vector bundles through their transition cocycles.
We study this construction for the alternating-map bifunctor $\Alt^k$ over a nontrivially normed field $K$.
The bifunctor is always $C^\infty$ and is analytic exactly when $k!\ne0$ in $K$.
Restricting to nonarchimedean spherically complete targets gives analyticity in every degree.
Even when the bifunctor is not analytic on whole hom spaces, it preserves analytic bundles over bases
with open $K^d$ charts, in every characteristic and with arbitrary normed fibers.

For scalar-valued alternating maps we obtain a sharper classification. Over a complete nonarchimedean field,
$\Alt^k(-;K)$ is analytic exactly when $k!\ne0$ or $K$ is spherically complete.
In the remaining cases, explicit Banach source spaces give scalar precomposition analytic at no point.
Polynomial shear coordinate changes turn this obstruction into failure of the naturally induced atlas on
an alternating cotangent bundle. An analytic scalar form can also have a polynomial pullback whose
alternating-valued coefficient map is nowhere analytic. Nevertheless, analytic sections of the multilinear
bundle with alternating values define a chart-independent calculus of forms, with pullback, shuffle wedge,
and exterior differentiation in every characteristic.

For general targets, Banach counterexamples exist over every field of positive characteristic once $k! = 0$.
Over $\F_q((u))$, finite coordinates are sharp among nonarchimedean Banach model spaces if all complete
targets are admitted. We also show that over $\F_p(t)$ there is no largest full subcategory on which
$\Alt^k$ is analytic when $k\ge p$. The original operator results and several family, bundle and categorical
theorems are formalized in Lean with Mathlib. The new scalar obstruction and the manifold-level calculus of
ambient analytic forms are proved here but are not yet formalized.
\end{abstract}
\maketitle

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}

An analytic change of coordinates on a Banach manifold need not act analytically on alternating covectors.
We construct such examples over complete fields of positive characteristic, with scalar-valued forms.
The obstruction concerns the natural alternating cotangent bundle: its induced transition maps satisfy
the cocycle identity, yet fail to be analytic.

This is a question about the passage from functors on vector spaces to constructions on vector bundles.
Let $\Alt^k(E;F)$ denote the space of bounded alternating $k$\nobreakdash-linear maps $E^k\to F$,
over a nontrivially normed field $K$. We prove that the action of $\Alt^k$ on spaces of bounded operators
is always $C^\infty$, and is analytic exactly when $k!\ne0$ in $K$.
A bundle construction, however, only requires analyticity along its transition families.
We show that families parametrized by open subsets of $K^d$ remain analytic in every characteristic
and every degree. Thus alternating maps preserve analytic vector bundles over such bases even when
the functor is not analytic on whole operator spaces.

To make the bundle question precise, let $M$ be a $C^n$ manifold over $K$, where
$n\in\N\cup\{\infty,\omega\}$ and $C^\omega$ means analytic, and let $\mathcal E,\mathcal H\to M$ be
$C^n$ vector bundles with typical fibers $E$ and $F$. Over a common trivializing cover $(U_i)$, their transition maps
$g_{ij}\colon U_i\cap U_j\to\operatorname{Aut}_K(E)$ and $h_{ij}\colon U_i\cap U_j\to\operatorname{Aut}_K(F)$
are $C^n$, with regularity measured in the operator norm; here $g_{ij}$ converts coordinates in chart $j$ to
coordinates in chart $i$. A bundle with fibers $\Alt^k(\mathcal E_x;\mathcal H_x)$ compatible with these
trivializations must then have the transition maps
\[
\theta_{ij}(x)\colon m\longmapsto h_{ij}(x)\circ m\circ\bigl(g_{ji}(x),\dots,g_{ji}(x)\bigr),\qquad m\in\Alt^k(E;F).
\]
The inverse transitions $g_{ji}=g_{ij}^{-1}$ belong to the cocycle data and are already $C^n$.
Functoriality gives the cocycle identity for $\theta_{ij}$, so it remains to establish their $C^n$ dependence
on $x$. Regularity on whole hom spaces is sufficient; for a given bundle, regularity along its transition
families is enough. Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction} gives the precise construction; compare
Lang's functors of class $C^p$~\cite[Ch.~III, \S4]{La}.
Taking $\mathcal E=TM$ and $\mathcal H=M\times K$ gives the scalar alternating cotangent bundle discussed above.
Over a complete field, this failure does not prevent a calculus of analytic differential forms.
Section~\ref{sec:scalar-forms} defines these as analytic sections of the multilinear bundle with alternating
values, with pullback, shuffle wedge products and exterior differentiation.
In characteristic zero, Gou\"ezel proves analyticity by polarization~\cite{G1} and uses it in the construction
of alternating-map bundles~\cite{G2}. Polarization divides by $k!$, which vanishes in $K$ exactly when $K$ has
characteristic $p>0$ and $k\ge p$.

Throughout, \emph{regular} means of class $C^n$ for one of the orders above.
Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-positive} proves the unconditional $C^\infty$ result, following the argument
formalized in the draft Mathlib contribution~\cite{PR}. Consequently the construction gives $C^n$
alternating-map bundles over every base for every $n\le\infty$, without completeness or dimension
assumptions (Corollary~\ref{cor:smooth-bundles}). The restrictions studied here arise at $n=\omega$.

We treat this question in three steps, which form the program of the paper. The first step shows that
analyticity of the bifunctor, on whole hom spaces or only along the transition families, makes the
alternating-map bundle analytic (Sections~\ref{sec:functor-bundles} and~\ref{sec:families-bundles}). The second
step uses a shear construction to turn a failure of analyticity on hom spaces into analytic input bundles whose
alternating-map bundle, with the transition maps above, is not analytic (Section~\ref{sec:nonanalytic-bundles}).
The third step seeks a largest analytic domain, that is, a largest class of pairs of spaces on which the
bifunctor is analytic, and studies the universal targets, the targets $F$ for which $\Alt^k(-;F)$ is analytic on
all hom spaces (Section~\ref{sec:domains}). The Main Theorem below records the outcome of each step.

\subsection*{Categories and regularity}
Fix a nontrivially normed field $K$. Let $\mathsf{Vec}_K$ be the category of normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces and
bounded linear maps, and let $\mathsf{Ban}_K$ be its full subcategory of complete spaces.
When $K$ has positive characteristic, write $\mathsf{Vec}_K^\circ$ for the full subcategory of nonarchimedean,
spherically complete spaces. The field itself need not be complete.

For an integer $k\ge0$, let $\Alt^k(E;F)$ be the normed space of bounded alternating $k$\nobreakdash-linear maps $E^k\to F$.
Here \emph{alternating} means vanishing whenever two arguments coincide, including in characteristic $2$.
Pullback in $E$ and pushforward in $F$ define the bifunctor
\[
\Alt^k\colon\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Vec}_K\longrightarrow\mathsf{Vec}_K,
\qquad (E,F)\longmapsto\Alt^k(E;F).
\]
Its action on a pair of morphisms $u\colon E\to E'$ and $v\colon F\to F'$ is
\[
\Alt^k(u,v)\colon m\longmapsto v\circ m\circ(u,\dots,u).
\]
The opposite category in the first variable records pullback. Write $\cL(E,E')$ for the hom space of bounded
linear maps, equipped with the operator norm.
We call the bifunctor \emph{analytic}, or \emph{$C^n$} for a given $n\in\N\cup\{\infty\}$, when every induced map on hom spaces
\[
\cL(E,E')\times\cL(F,F')\longrightarrow
\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E';F),\Alt^k(E;F')\bigr)
\]
has that regularity. The product carries the maximum norm. This definition concerns the joint action on both
variables, rather than separate regularity. It applies to the restrictions below as well.

\subsection*{The operator form}
The proofs work with the action of a fixed-target functor $\Alt^k(-;F)$ on hom spaces.
For normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces $E,E',F$ and a fixed degree $k\ge0$, the pullback action is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Q}
\begin{aligned}
A=A^k_{E,E';F}\colon\cL(E,E')&\longrightarrow\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E';F),\Alt^k(E;F)\bigr),\\
A(f)(m)&=m\circ(f,\dots,f).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Thus $A^k_{E,E';F}(u)=\Alt^k(u,\id_F)$. We abbreviate this action to $A$ when the
spaces and degree are fixed, and write $A^{k,K}_{E,E';F}$ when the base field must be specified.
For multilinear maps without the alternation condition, precomposition is the diagonal of a bounded multilinear
map and is therefore analytic. Requiring the values to remain alternating changes the problem: polarization is
unavailable when $k!=0$ in $K$, and over Banach spaces this obstruction cannot be avoided (Theorem~\ref{thm:main}). For $k\ge1$, the lift criterion
(Proposition~\ref{prop:lift}) shows that $A$ is analytic at one point if and only if it is analytic at every
point, and that this holds if and only if $A$ has a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift
(Definition~\ref{def:lift}).

The translation between operators and functors uses only bounded bilinear postcomposition. Write $v_*a=v\circ a$.
The map $(v,T)\mapsto v_*\circ T$ is bounded bilinear, with $\norm{v_*\circ T}\le\norm v\,\norm T$, and for
$u\in\cL(E,E')$ and $v\in\cL(F,F')$ the joint action of the bifunctor is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:joint-action}
\Alt^k(u,v)=v_*\circ A^k_{E,E';F}(u).
\end{equation}
Consequently $C^n$ regularity or analyticity of every $A$ gives the same regularity of the bifunctor.
Conversely, restriction to $v=\id_F$ recovers $A$. A bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift $P$ of $A$ gives the
bounded $(k+1)$\nobreakdash-linear map $(u_1,\dots,u_k,v)\mapsto v_*\circ P(u_1,\dots,u_k)$, with the pushforward
variable in the last slot and value $\Alt^k(u,v)$ at $(u,\dots,u,v)$, so finite-power-series conclusions pass from
$A$ to the bifunctor.

The space $\Alt^k(E;F)$ is complete whenever $F$ is complete: an operator-norm Cauchy sequence converges
pointwise in $F$, and its boundedness, multilinearity and alternation pass to the limit. Hence the bifunctor
restricts to $\Alt^k\colon\mathsf{Ban}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Ban}_K\to\mathsf{Ban}_K$. In degree zero,
$\Alt^0(E;F)$ is naturally isometric to $F$; pullback is constant and the joint action is linear in the
pushforward variable.

The two notions used above to define $\mathsf{Vec}_K^\circ$ also enter the Main Theorem below. A norm is \emph{nonarchimedean} if
$\norm{x+y}\le\max(\norm x,\norm y)$, and likewise for the absolute value of $K$. A metric space is
\emph{spherically complete} if every nonempty family of closed balls that meet pairwise has a common point;
\S\ref{sec:theoremA} gives the details. Every normed field of characteristic $p>0$ is nonarchimedean
(Lemma~\ref{lem:ultra}). Over a nonarchimedean $K$, if $F$ is nonarchimedean and spherically complete then so
is $\Alt^k(E;F)$ for every normed $E$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:alt-spherical}); this closure property is the
codomain assertion of Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}) below.

\needspace{11\baselineskip}
\subsection*{Main results}
\begin{thmMain}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field and let $k\ge0$ be an integer.
\begin{enumerate}
\renewcommand{\labelenumi}{\textup{(\arabic{enumi})}}
\item\label{main:general} The bifunctor
\[
\Alt^k\colon\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Vec}_K\longrightarrow\mathsf{Vec}_K
\]
is $C^\infty$. It is analytic if and only if $k!\ne0$ in $K$. Both assertions hold also for its restriction
\[
\Alt^k\colon\mathsf{Ban}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Ban}_K\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ban}_K.
\]
\item\label{main:spherical} If $K$ has positive characteristic, the restriction
\[
\Alt^k\colon\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Vec}_K^\circ
\longrightarrow\mathsf{Vec}_K^\circ
\]
is analytic in every degree.
\item\label{main:scalar} Suppose $K$ is complete and nonarchimedean. The contravariant functor
\[
\Alt^k(-;K)\colon\mathsf{Ban}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ban}_K
\]
is analytic if and only if $k!\ne0$ in $K$ or $K$ is spherically complete. The same criterion holds on
$\mathsf{Vec}_K$ and with any fixed nonzero finite-dimensional value space in place of $K$.
If both alternatives fail, the naturally induced scalar alternating cotangent bundle of a suitable
analytic Banach manifold has an operator-valued transition family that is analytic at no point.
\item\label{main:families} With no restriction on $K$ or $k$, the bifunctor $\Alt^k$ preserves every analytic
family of morphisms parametrized by an open subset of $K^d$. Consequently it induces a bifunctor on
analytic normed vector bundles over any analytic manifold with open $K^d$ charts, preserving
operator-valued analytic bundle morphisms. No completeness or dimension hypothesis on the fibers is required.
\item\label{main:failure} If $k!=0$ in $K$, the bundle conclusion of~(\ref{main:families}) does not extend to
all Banach bases. There are an analytic manifold $M$ modeled on a Banach space, with a single chart, and analytic
normed vector bundles over $M$ whose alternating-map bundle, formed with the induced transition maps as
in~(\ref{main:families}), has a transition map that is analytic at no point of $M$. The two trivializations
involved differ by an affine analytic family of invertible operators with affine analytic inverse.
\item\label{main:forms} Over every complete $K$, ambient analytic scalar forms define a contravariant
functor from $C^\omega$ normed manifolds and maps to differential graded $K$\nobreakdash-algebras.
Here ambient analyticity is
measured in the multilinear bundle, with values required to be alternating. If the inclusion of alternating
maps into multilinear maps on the model space has a bounded linear retraction, these forms agree with
analytic sections of the natural alternating bundle. When $K$ is complete and not spherically complete
and $k! = 0$, polynomial pullback need not preserve intrinsic alternating-valued analyticity, even in
standard product trivializations of Banach spaces.
\item\label{main:domains} For every prime $p$ and every $k\ge p$, over the $t$\nobreakdash-adically normed
field $K=\F_p(t)$ there is no largest full subcategory of
$\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Vec}_K$ on which $\Alt^k$ is analytic.
This remains true for subcategories closed under isomorphisms and, within the corresponding ambient category,
when all objects have finite algebraic dimension and nonarchimedean norms.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thmMain}
Items~(\ref{main:general}), (\ref{main:spherical}) and~(\ref{main:families}) contain the positive results of the first step. The analytic
assertion of item~(\ref{main:general}) gives the exact range $k<p$ in characteristic $p>0$, and every degree in
characteristic zero; the positive direction is Proposition~\ref{prop:factorial-positive}.
Item~(\ref{main:spherical}) removes the restriction on the degree by restricting the target category, and no
completeness of $K$ is needed. In the analytic cases of items~(\ref{main:general}) and~(\ref{main:spherical}),
the maps on hom spaces are given by finite power series. Over a complete field, $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is also analytic in
every degree when $E$ or $E'$ is finite-dimensional (Corollary~\ref{cor:finite-dimensional-positive}), and a
finite-dimensional target reduces the question to scalar-valued forms (Remark~\ref{rem:finite-target-reduction}).
Item~(\ref{main:scalar}) settles that scalar question over complete fields: the only extra positive case,
beyond the factorial range, is spherical completeness of $K$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-classification}).
Its negative direction uses nonarchimedean source spaces and the one-dimensional target $K$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-counter}); thus it differs essentially from the general-target counterexample.
For bundles, analytic input bundles give an analytic alternating-map bundle over every analytic base when $k!\ne0$
in $K$, when $K$ is nonarchimedean and the target fibers are nonarchimedean and spherically complete, or when the
source fibers have finite continuous coordinates (Corollary~\ref{cor:analytic-bundles}). Over bases with open $K^d$
charts, item~(\ref{main:families}) removes every condition on the characteristic, the degree and the fibers
(Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-bundles}). Its proof applies analytic reflection through a closed linear isometry to
the transition families themselves, using that their parameters are finite coordinates
(Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection}). Bases modeled on $\ell^1(I,K)$ also admit the construction when the
target fibers are complete, and over a nonarchimedean field so do bases modeled on $c_0(I,K)$ when the target fibers
are complete and nonarchimedean (Corollaries~\ref{fam:cor:l1-families} and~\ref{fam:cor:c0-families}).

Item~(\ref{main:failure}) is the outcome of the second step. Like the negative direction of
item~(\ref{main:general}), it rests on the counterexample theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:main}): for every $K$ of
characteristic $p>0$ and every $k\ge p$, there are Banach spaces $E,F$ such that $A^k_{E,E;F}$ is analytic at no
point, where $F$ admits no equivalent nonarchimedean norm. Corollary~\ref{cor:class} combines
Theorem~\ref{thm:main} with the factorial case, and the shear construction (Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:shear}) turns
it into Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:banach-base-failure}, which is item~(\ref{main:failure}). This is a failure of the
natural construction under analytic changes of trivialization, not a claim that the pointwise bundle admits no
analytic structure, and the $C^n$ analogues hold over every base for $n\in\N\cup\{\infty\}$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:smooth-bundles}). The base need not be an operator space: over $K=\F_q((u))$, with $q$ a power
of $p$ and $k\ge p$, the open unit ball of $c_0(\N,K)$ with diagonal isometric transitions already fails
(Corollary~\ref{lau:cor:diagonal-bundle}). Among Banach model spaces over this field admitting an equivalent
nonarchimedean norm, finite dimension is therefore exactly the right hypothesis on the base
(Corollary~\ref{lau:cor:dichotomy}). Over the incomplete field $\F_p(t)$, with $k\ge p$, a base of finite algebraic
dimension without continuous coordinates also fails (Remark~\ref{dom:finite-algebraic-base}).

The scalar obstruction in item~(\ref{main:scalar}) also occurs for tangent bundles themselves:
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-tangent} realizes it by the derivative of a polynomial shear diffeomorphism.
Item~(\ref{main:forms}) supplies a calculus of analytic forms despite this failure
(Proposition~\ref{prop:ambient-calculus}). The distinction from intrinsic alternating-valued analyticity is
real: Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-pullback} gives a polynomial pullback that is ambiently analytic but intrinsically
nowhere analytic. Agreement follows under the retraction hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:ambient-comparison};
existence of an analytic product-bundle structure alone does not imply it.

Item~(\ref{main:domains}) is the outcome of the third step. Every analytic domain (Definition~\ref{dom:definition})
yields analytic alternating-map bundles over every analytic base, for input bundles whose pair of typical fibers is
an object of the domain (Corollary~\ref{cor:domain-bundles}). The pairs whose target $F$ is universal, in the sense
that $\Alt^k(-;F)$ is analytic on all hom spaces, form the universal-target core, and adjoining its objects to any
analytic domain gives an analytic domain (Proposition~\ref{dom:core}). Nevertheless no largest analytic domain exists
over $\F_p(t)$ for $k\ge p$ (Theorem~\ref{dom:no-largest}). Over incomplete fields, finite algebraic dimension alone
does not supply continuous coordinates; this is precisely what permits these examples. Item~(\ref{main:domains})
does not decide the corresponding question over complete fields or among Banach pairs.

\subsection*{Related results}
The characteristic-zero polarization argument of~\cite{G1} works whenever $k!\ne0$ in $K$; we prove it in
\S\ref{sec:positive}. Theorem~\ref{thm:main} shows that the regularity range $n\le\infty$ in the draft Mathlib
contribution~\cite{PR} is sharp over every field of characteristic $p>0$ once $k\ge p$: in these examples,
Mathlib's \texttt{ContDiffAt K $\omega$} fails at every point.

The answer is different if the target norm is required to be nonarchimedean. If $K$ is nonarchimedean and $F$ is
nonarchimedean and spherically complete, then $A$ is analytic for all normed $E,E'$ (Main Theorem). The proof,
outlined in \S\ref{sec:theoremA} and given in Appendix~\ref{app:spherical}, constructs a contracting retraction from
multilinear maps to alternating maps by spherical completeness and extension of bounded linear maps. This positive
result is independent of the counterexample theorem and Corollary~\ref{cor:class}. A special case appears
in~\cite[Remark~16]{M}. Over a discretely valued base, the Main Theorem in fact rules out a Banach counterexample
whenever $F$ has an equivalent nonarchimedean norm (Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete}).

The earlier characteristic-$2$ construction~\cite{M} uses a nonarchimedean target and gives nonanalyticity at $0$
for $E=E'=\linf$ and $k=2$ over every complete nonarchimedean field of characteristic $2$ that is not spherically
complete. Its formal hypothesis, a descending sequence of closed balls with empty intersection, is equivalent to
failure of spherical completeness (Remark~\ref{rem:K2}). The higher-characteristic cases were left open there.
The present construction works over every nontrivially normed field of positive characteristic, including
spherically complete fields, by allowing targets with no equivalent nonarchimedean norm.
The new scalar construction of Appendix~\ref{app:scalar} instead combines a finite-dimensional obstruction
with a gap between families of chains. Its only nonspherical-completeness input is the arbitrary-arity
multilinear tail theorem from~\cite{M}, which has no characteristic-two hypothesis; the underlying scalar
duality is due to van der Put~\cite[Theorems~3.2 and~4.1]{VdP}.
The remaining questions for nonarchimedean targets are discussed in \S\ref{sec:complements}.

\subsection*{Organization and verification scope}
After the motivation above, the paper carries out the three steps of the program; Section~\ref{sec:preliminaries}
first fixes conventions and states the lift criterion. The first step, from analytic functors to analytic bundles,
occupies Section~\ref{sec:functor-bundles}, on whole hom spaces, and Section~\ref{sec:families-bundles}, along
analytic families. The second, from a nonanalytic functor to a nonanalytic bundle through the shear, is
Section~\ref{sec:nonanalytic-bundles}. Section~\ref{sec:scalar-forms} refines this step for scalar targets
and develops the ambient calculus of analytic forms. The third, the search for the largest analytic domain, is
Section~\ref{sec:domains}. Section~\ref{sec:open} collects open problems. The long technical proofs are deferred to
appendices, in the order of the main text, so that it can be read without them:
Appendices~\ref{app:lift}--\ref{app:families} for the lift criterion and the first step,
Appendices~\ref{app:FF}--\ref{app:assembly} for Theorem~\ref{thm:main}, Appendix~\ref{app:scalar} for
the scalar counterexample, and Appendix~\ref{app:no-largest} for Theorem~\ref{dom:no-largest}. Appendix~\ref{app:further} collects further complements on nonarchimedean targets.

The counterexample theorem and the factorial classification (Theorem~\ref{thm:main} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:class}), including their supporting finite-field and functional-analytic arguments, are
formalized in Lean~4 with Mathlib. The checked development also contains the operator form of
Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}), the discrete-target result and the sorted-basis lift.
The analytic-family, bundle and no-largest-domain results are later extensions. Their mathematical proofs are
included here; Appendix~\ref{app:lean} and the repository's theorem map give their separate formalization status.
In particular, Main Theorem~(\ref{main:scalar}), the scalar bundle and pullback counterexamples, and the
manifold-level assertions of item~(\ref{main:forms}) are new informal results. The scalar obstruction itself
has not yet been checked in Lean; the earlier tail input and four vector-space form lemmas have been
independently recompiled.

\section{Analytic preliminaries}\label{sec:preliminaries}\label{sec:prelim}

The lift criterion reduces the analytic question to a bounded multilinear identity. Its proof uses uniqueness
on scalar lines and applies in every characteristic, without completeness assumptions.

We first fix the conventions used throughout and then state the lift criterion, Proposition~\ref{prop:lift}. The one-variable uniqueness of power-series coefficients on which it rests is Lemma~\ref{lem:unique}, and both proofs are given in Appendix~\ref{app:lift}.

\subsection{Conventions}
We write $\N=\{0,1,2,\dots\}$. Throughout, $K$ denotes the base field and $k$ the degree. A \emph{normed field} $K$ carries a multiplicative absolute value $\abs{\cdot}$ satisfying the triangle inequality. It is
\emph{nontrivially normed} if some $c\in K$ has $0<\abs c<1$, and \emph{nonarchimedean} if $\abs{x+y}\le\max(\abs x,\abs y)$.
A \emph{normed $K$\nobreakdash-space} $X$ satisfies $\norm{\lambda x}=\abs\lambda\norm x$; it is \emph{Banach} if it is complete.

We write $\cL(X,Y)$ for the bounded
linear maps, $\Mult^k(X;Y)$ for the bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear maps $X^k\to Y$, and $\Alt^k(X;Y)\subseteq\Mult^k(X;Y)$ for the
closed subspace of alternating maps, all with the operator norm. A subscript $K$ records the field when needed.
An alternating map $m$ is antisymmetric: expanding $0=m(\dots,y+z,\dots,y+z,\dots)$ gives
$m(\dots,y,\dots,z,\dots)=-m(\dots,z,\dots,y,\dots)$, and hence
$m(x_{\sigma(1)},\dots,x_{\sigma(k)})=\sgn(\sigma)\,m(x_1,\dots,x_k)$ for $\sigma\in S_k$. In characteristic $2$ the
converse fails.

We index the $k$ arguments by $\{1,\dots,k\}$ throughout. Reindexing by any finite set with $k$ elements is an
isometric isomorphism of alternating-map spaces and intertwines precomposition. Thus the results also apply to
the arbitrary finite index types used in Lean, with the same counterexample spaces. For each fixed degree we write
\[
A=A^k_{E,E';F}\colon\cL(E,E')\to\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E';F),\Alt^k(E;F)\bigr),
\]
where $A(f)(m)(x_1,\dots,x_k)=m(fx_1,\dots,fx_k)$.

\begin{definition}\label{def:lift}
A \emph{bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift} of $A$ is a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear map
$P\colon\cL(E,E')^k\to\cL(\Alt^k(E';F),\Alt^k(E;F))$ with $P(f,\dots,f)=A(f)$ for all $f\in\cL(E,E')$.
\end{definition}

A map $g\colon X\to Z$ between normed spaces is \emph{analytic at} $x_0$ if there are bounded $n$\nobreakdash-linear maps
$p_n\colon X^n\to Z$ ($n\ge0$) and a radius $R>0$ with $\sup_n\norm{p_n}R^n<\infty$ such that
$g(x_0+h)=\sum_{n\ge0}p_n(h,\dots,h)$, a convergent series, whenever $\norm h<R$. Up to shrinking $R$ this is
Mathlib's \texttt{AnalyticAt}~\cite{docs}, and in Mathlib \texttt{ContDiffAt K $\omega$} at a point implies
\texttt{AnalyticAt} there (\texttt{ContDiffAt.analyticAt}).

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:ultra}
A normed field of characteristic $p>0$ is nonarchimedean.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Frobenius and the triangle inequality give, for every $m\ge0$,
\[
 \abs{x+y}^{p^m}=\abs{x^{p^m}+y^{p^m}}
 \le2\max(\abs x,\abs y)^{p^m}.
\]
Taking $p^m$\nobreakdash-th roots and letting $m\to\infty$ proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The lift criterion}
A power series for $A$ at any base point determines a lift through its degree-$k$ term. The only uniqueness
statement needed is one-dimensional, so the argument does not use a polarization formula or division by $k!$.

\begin{proposition}[lift criterion]\label{prop:lift}
Let $K$ be any nontrivially normed field, $E,E',F$ normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces and $k\ge1$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $A$ has a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift $P$, then at every $f_0\in\cL(E,E')$ the map $A$ is given by a finite power
series of infinite radius; in particular $A$ is analytic at every point.
\item If $A$ is analytic at some point $f_0$, with power series $(p_n)_n$ there, then $p_k$ is a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear
lift of $A$.
\item Hence the following are equivalent: $A$ has a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift; $A$ is analytic at every point; $A$ is
analytic at some point. If they fail, then Mathlib's \texttt{ContDiffAt K $\omega$ A $f_0$} fails at every $f_0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:lift}.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:liftlean}
The degree-$k$ term of \emph{every} power series representing $A$ is a lift, regardless of the base point.
This does not assert uniqueness of the multilinear lift: in positive characteristic, different multilinear maps
can have the same diagonal.
\end{remark}

\section{From regular functors to regular bundles}\label{sec:functor-bundles}\label{sec:positive}

This section works on whole hom spaces. Regularity of a functor on hom spaces passes to the vector bundles it
induces (Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction}). The alternating functor is $C^\infty$ in every
characteristic (Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-positive}), because smoothness descends from all multilinear maps
through a closed isometric inclusion. Three constructions give bounded multilinear lifts of $A^k_{E,E';F}$, and
hence analyticity by Proposition~\ref{prop:lift}: division by $k!$ when it is nonzero in $K$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:factorial-positive}); determinant formulas when $E$ or $E'$ has a finite basis with
continuous coordinate functionals (Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-coordinate}); and, over a nonarchimedean field, a
contractive retraction onto alternating maps when $F$ is nonarchimedean and spherically complete
(\S\ref{sec:theoremA} and Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical})). Corollary~\ref{cor:analytic-bundles} collects the
consequences for alternating-map bundles.

\subsection{Bundles from regular functors}\label{ssec:bundle-construction}
Fix $n\in\N\cup\{\infty,\omega\}$, where $C^\omega$ means analytic, and let $M$ be a $C^n$ manifold over $K$,
modeled on any normed $K$\nobreakdash-space.
A $C^n$ vector bundle $\mathcal V\to M$ with typical fiber $V$ is given by an open cover $(U_i)$ of $M$ and $C^n$
transition maps $g_{ij}\colon U_i\cap U_j\to\operatorname{Aut}_K(V)\subseteq\End_K(V)=\cL(V,V)$. Here
$\operatorname{Aut}_K(V)$ consists of bounded linear isomorphisms with bounded inverse, and regularity is measured
in the operator norm. The map $g_{ij}$ converts coordinates in chart $j$ to coordinates in chart $i$, and
$g_{ii}(x)=\id_V$ and $g_{ij}(x)\circ g_{j\ell}(x)=g_{i\ell}(x)$ on the relevant overlaps. Conversely, such a cocycle
glues the products $U_i\times V$ into a $C^n$ vector bundle: the coordinate changes $(x,v)\mapsto(x,g_{ij}(x)v)$
are $C^n$ because operator evaluation is bounded bilinear. In particular $g_{ji}=g_{ij}^{-1}$, and both
directions are $C^n$ by the cocycle data.

A multifunctor of mixed variance in $r$ variables is a functor
$\mathcal F\colon\mathsf C_1\times\cdots\times\mathsf C_r\to\mathsf{Vec}_K$, where each $\mathsf C_a$ is
$\mathsf{Vec}_K$ or $\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}$. As for $\Alt^k$, it is $C^n$ when its induced maps on products
of hom spaces, with the maximum norm, are jointly $C^n$. For transition maps
$\mathbf g_{ij}=(g^1_{ij},\dots,g^r_{ij})$ we write $\mathcal F(\mathbf g_{ij}(x))$ for $\mathcal F$ applied to
$g^a_{ij}(x)$ in each covariant variable and to $g^a_{ji}(x)$ in each contravariant variable.

\begin{proposition}[bundles from regular functors]\label{prop:bundle-construction}
Let $n\in\N\cup\{\infty,\omega\}$, let $M$ be a $C^n$ manifold over $K$, and let $\mathcal F$ be a multifunctor of
mixed variance in $r$ variables. Let $\mathcal V_1,\dots,\mathcal V_r$ be $C^n$ vector bundles over $M$ with a common
trivializing cover, typical fibers $V_a$ and transition maps $g^a_{ij}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Suppose that $\mathcal F$ is $C^n$. Then the maps $\theta_{ij}(x)=\mathcal F(\mathbf g_{ij}(x))$ form a $C^n$
cocycle with values in $\End_K(\mathcal F(V_1,\dots,V_r))$. It defines a $C^n$ vector bundle
$\mathcal F(\mathcal V_1,\dots,\mathcal V_r)$ with fiber $\mathcal F((\mathcal V_1)_x,\dots,(\mathcal V_r)_x)$ over $x$.
Up to canonical isomorphism this bundle is independent of the cover and the trivializations, and it is
functorial, with the variance of $\mathcal F$, in $C^n$ bundle morphisms.
\item More generally, the same conclusions hold without assuming that $\mathcal F$ is $C^n$, provided the maps
$x\mapsto\mathcal F(\mathbf g_{ij}(x))$ are $C^n$, and so are the corresponding composites of $\mathcal F$ with
the changes of trivialization and with the local expressions of bundle morphisms.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Functoriality gives $\theta_{ii}(x)=\id$ and $\theta_{ij}(x)\circ\theta_{j\ell}(x)=\mathcal F(\mathbf g_{ij}(x)\circ\mathbf g_{j\ell}(x))
=\theta_{i\ell}(x)$, the composite in a contravariant variable being $g^a_{\ell j}(x)\circ g^a_{ji}(x)=g^a_{\ell i}(x)$.
In case~(1), $\theta_{ij}$ is $C^n$ as the composite of $x\mapsto\mathbf g_{ij}(x)$ with the $C^n$ map induced by
$\mathcal F$ on hom spaces; in case~(2), this is the hypothesis. The inverse transitions used in contravariant
variables are already $C^n$ by the cocycle data, so no separate regularity theorem for inversion is needed.
Applying $\mathcal F$ in the same way to the $C^n$ operator-valued local expressions of changes of trivialization
and of bundle morphisms gives $C^n$ local expressions, by composition in case~(1) and by hypothesis in case~(2).
They are compatible with the cocycles $\theta_{ij}$ by functoriality. This identifies the bundles obtained from
different choices and gives the functoriality in bundle morphisms.
\end{proof}
In particular, a multifunctor may be applied repeatedly to the same bundle.

For $\mathcal F=\Alt^k$, let $\mathcal E,\mathcal H\to M$ have typical fibers $E,F$ and transition maps
$g_{ij},h_{ij}$, so that $\mathbf g_{ij}=(g_{ij},h_{ij})$ with $\mathcal E$ in the contravariant variable. The cocycle
of Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction} for $\Alt^k(\mathcal E;\mathcal H)$ is
\[
\theta_{ij}(x)\colon m\longmapsto h_{ij}(x)\circ m\circ\bigl(g_{ji}(x),\dots,g_{ji}(x)\bigr),\qquad m\in\Alt^k(E;F).
\]
By~\eqref{eq:joint-action}, $\theta_{ij}(x)$ is obtained from $A^k_{E,E;F}(g_{ji}(x))$ by postcomposition with
$h_{ij}(x)$, which is bounded bilinear in the pair. These transitions are therefore $C^n$ as soon as
$A^k_{E,E;F}$ is $C^n$, and bundle morphisms between bundles with different typical fibers require the same of
$A^k_{E,E';F}$. Part~(2) of the proposition is the version used in Corollaries~\ref{cor:analytic-bundles} and~\ref{cor:domain-bundles},
which need analyticity only on the hom spaces between fibers satisfying their hypotheses, and in
Section~\ref{sec:families-bundles}, where analyticity is established only along transition families. The rest of this section supplies the regularity of
$A$ on whole hom spaces.

\subsection{Smoothness in every characteristic}
We first record why finite-order differentiability descends through a closed isometric inclusion. The argument
also explains why smoothness survives when a multilinear analytic lift does not.

\begin{lemma}[smoothness through a closed isometric inclusion]\label{lem:smooth-reflection}
Let $U,V,W$ be normed spaces over a nontrivially normed field $K$, and let $j\colon V\to W$ be a linear isometry
with closed range. For a map $g\colon U\to V$ and $n\in\N\cup\{\infty\}$, the map $g$ is $C^n$ if and only if $j\circ g$
is $C^n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One direction follows by composition with a continuous linear map. For the converse, continuity follows from the
isometry. Suppose $j\circ g$ has Fr\'echet derivative $A\colon U\to W$ at $x$. For each $h\in U$,
\[
Ah=\lim_{t\to0,\ t\ne0}\frac{j(g(x+th))-j(g(x))}{t}.
\]
The quotients lie in $j(V)$, and nontriviality of the norm supplies nonzero scalars tending to zero. Since $j(V)$ is
closed, $Ah\in j(V)$. Thus $A=j\circ B$ for a unique linear map $B\colon U\to V$. The identity
$\norm{Bh}=\norm{Ah}$ makes $B$ bounded, and the isometry transfers the Fr\'echet remainder estimate from $A$ to
$B$. Consequently $g$ is differentiable and
\[
D(j\circ g)=j_*\circ Dg,\qquad
j_*\colon\cL(U,V)\longrightarrow\cL(U,W),\quad T\longmapsto j\circ T.
\]
The map $j_*$ is again a linear isometry with closed range. Indeed, an operator belongs to its range exactly when
all its values lie in $j(V)$; this is a closed condition, being an intersection of inverse images under continuous
evaluation maps. Applying the same argument to $j_*$ proves the assertion for $C^{n+1}$ from the assertion for
$C^n$. Induction proves every finite order, and hence $C^\infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[smoothness of precomposition]\label{prop:smooth-positive}
For every nontrivially normed field $K$, all normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces $E,E',F$, and every integer $k\ge0$, the map
$A^k_{E,E';F}$ is $C^n$ for every $n\in\N\cup\{\infty\}$. In particular, precomposition is always $C^\infty$,
without completeness, finite-dimensionality, or characteristic assumptions.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The inclusion
$\Alt^k(E;F)\hookrightarrow\Mult^k(E;F)$ is isometric and has closed range: alternation is the intersection of the
closed conditions $m(x_1,\dots,x_k)=0$ for tuples with two equal entries. Postcomposition therefore gives a linear
isometry with closed range
\[
\mathcal I\colon\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E';F),\Alt^k(E;F)\bigr)
\longrightarrow\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E';F),\Mult^k(E;F)\bigr).
\]
In the larger target, precomposition is the diagonal of the bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear map
\[
\mathcal B(f_1,\dots,f_k)(m)(x_1,\dots,x_k)
=m(f_1x_1,\dots,f_kx_k),
\]
whose norm is at most $1$. Hence $\mathcal I\circ A$ is a continuous polynomial and is $C^\infty$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:smooth-reflection} gives the result. The degree-zero case is again constant.
\end{proof}

This is the argument formalized in~\cite{PR}. It uses closedness to recover derivatives, rather than a projection
onto alternating maps. The induction proves all finite orders but does not recover analytic coefficients in
positive characteristic. Theorem~\ref{thm:main} shows that the resulting $C^\infty$ conclusion cannot in general be
strengthened to $C^\omega$.

Combined with the cocycle construction, Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-positive} settles every finite order of differentiability and the case $n=\infty$.

\begin{corollary}[smooth alternating-map bundles]\label{cor:smooth-bundles}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field, let $k\ge0$ be an integer, let $n\in\N\cup\{\infty\}$, and let $M$ be a
$C^n$ manifold over $K$ modeled on any normed space. Fiberwise alternating maps define a bifunctor
\[
(\mathcal E,\mathcal H)\longmapsto\Alt^k(\mathcal E;\mathcal H),\qquad
\Alt^k(\mathcal E;\mathcal H)_x=\Alt^k(\mathcal E_x;\mathcal H_x),
\]
contravariant in the first variable, on $C^n$ normed vector bundles over $M$ with arbitrary normed typical fibers
and $C^n$ bundle morphisms. No completeness, dimension or characteristic hypothesis is needed.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-positive}, every $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is $C^n$. Since $(v,T)\mapsto v_*\circ T$ is bounded
bilinear, \eqref{eq:joint-action} shows that the joint action of $\Alt^k$ on hom spaces is $C^n$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction}(1) now applies.
\end{proof}

The remaining case is $n=\omega$, which occupies the rest of the paper.

\subsection{Invertible factorial: in particular, \texorpdfstring{$p>k$}{p > k}}
\begin{proposition}\label{prop:factorial-positive}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field and let $E,E',F$ be normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces. If $k!\ne0$ in $K$, then $A$
is given at every point by a finite power series of infinite radius. In particular, it is analytic whenever
$\operatorname{char}K=0$, or whenever $\operatorname{char}K=p>k$. No completeness or dimension hypothesis is needed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $k=0$, precomposition is constant. Suppose $k\ge1$ and define
\[
P(f_1,\dots,f_k)(m)(x_1,\dots,x_k)
:=\frac1{k!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_k}
m\bigl(f_{\sigma(1)}x_1,\dots,f_{\sigma(k)}x_k\bigr).
\]
This is multilinear in the $f_i$ and in the $x_j$, linear in $m$, and satisfies
\[
\norm{P(f_1,\dots,f_k)(m)(x_1,\dots,x_k)}
\le \abs{(k!)^{-1}}\,k!\,\norm m\prod_i\norm{f_i}\prod_j\norm{x_j},
\]
where the second $k!$ on the right is a real number counting the terms. To check alternation, suppose $x_a=x_b$ and
let $\tau$ exchange $a$ and $b$. The summands indexed by $\sigma$ and $\sigma\circ\tau$ cancel by antisymmetry of $m$.
This pairing is a fixed-point-free involution, so it proves vanishing also in characteristic $2$. Thus $P$ takes
values in the required space of alternating maps and is a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift: on the diagonal,
all $k!$ summands equal $A(f)(m)(x)$. Proposition~\ref{prop:lift} gives the claimed power series.

In a field of characteristic $p>0$, the scalar $k!$ is nonzero precisely when $k<p$. This gives the stated
positive-characteristic case.
\end{proof}

% Proposed finite-coordinate positive cases. This is new manuscript prose,
% not a claim that a corresponding new general theorem has been added to Lean.

\subsection{Finite-coordinate operator domains and codomains}

The finite-dimensional argument uses continuous coordinate functionals. We state this hypothesis explicitly so
that the result also applies over incomplete fields whenever the chosen finite-dimensional space has its usual
coordinate topology.

\begin{proposition}[finite coordinate bases]\label{prop:finite-coordinate}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field and let $E,E',F$ be normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces. If either $E$ or $E'$ has a
finite algebraic basis whose coordinate functionals are continuous, then $A^k_{E,E';F}$ has a bounded
$k$\nobreakdash-linear lift in every degree $k\ge1$. In particular, it is continuously polynomial, and hence analytic,
at every point. No completeness or nonarchimedean hypothesis on $F$ is required.
\end{proposition}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:lift}.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:findim}
Let $\mathbb K$ be a complete nontrivially normed field and $U$ a finite-dimensional normed $\mathbb K$\nobreakdash-space. Then every
linear functional on $U$ is bounded, and $U$ is complete.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We prove both assertions by induction on $n=\dim U$, the case $n=0$ being immediate. Suppose $n\ge1$ and let $g\ne0$
be a linear functional on $U$. Its kernel $H$ has dimension $n-1$, so the induction hypothesis makes $H$ complete
and therefore closed in $U$. Choose $v$ with $g(v)=1$. Since $v\notin H$,
$\delta:=\inf_{h\in H}\norm{v-h}>0$. Write $x=g(x)v+h$ with $h\in H$. If $g(x)\ne0$, then
\[
\norm x=\abs{g(x)}\,\norm{v+g(x)^{-1}h}\ge\abs{g(x)}\delta.
\]
Thus $\abs{g(x)}\le\delta^{-1}\norm x$ for every $x$, proving boundedness.

Choose a basis of $U$. Its coordinate functionals are now bounded, so the coordinate map
$U\to\mathbb K^n$, with the max norm on $\mathbb K^n$, is bounded. The inverse map
$(c_i)\mapsto\sum_ic_ie_i$ is bounded by $\sum_i\norm{e_i}$. Hence $U$ is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to the complete
space $\mathbb K^n$, and is itself complete.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:finite-dimensional-positive}
Let $K$ be a complete nontrivially normed field, and let $E,E',F$ be normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces. If either $E$ or $E'$
is finite-dimensional over $K$, then $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is analytic at every point in every degree. No completeness
assumption on $E,E'$ or $F$ is needed in the statement.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:findim} gives continuous coordinates on whichever of $E,E'$ is finite-dimensional.
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-coordinate} then applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the coordinate hypothesis over an incomplete field]
Finite algebraic dimension alone does not imply continuity of coordinate functionals over an incomplete field.
For example, choose $a\in\widehat K\setminus K$ and give $H:=K+Ka\subseteq\widehat K$ the induced norm. The vectors
$1,a$ form a $K$\nobreakdash-basis. If $a_j\in K$ tends to $a$, then $a-a_j\to0$ in $H$, whereas its $a$\nobreakdash-coordinate
is always $1$. Thus that coordinate functional is discontinuous. This prevents dropping the coordinate hypothesis
from the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-coordinate}; it does not by itself give a counterexample to analyticity of $A$ for such spaces.
Over an incomplete field the proposition still applies, for example, to finite-dimensional spaces whose norm is
equivalent to a coordinate maximum norm.
\end{remark}

Finite-dimensionality of the target $F$ leads to a different question. Remark~\ref{rem:finite-target-reduction}
reduces it to scalar-valued forms and gives a positive result over a spherically complete nonarchimedean base.

\subsection{Spherically complete targets}\label{sec:theoremA}
This subsection and Appendix~\ref{app:spherical} prove part~(\ref{main:spherical}) of the Main Theorem. Spherical completeness allows us
to project bounded multilinear maps onto alternating maps without increasing the norm. Applying this projection
to the ordinary multilinear precomposition formula produces an analytic lift in every characteristic and degree.
The argument is independent of the counterexample construction. The retraction strategy reflects the classical
role of spherically complete spaces as injective objects in nonarchimedean Banach-space theory;
see Schikhof~\cite[\S2.8]{Schi} for this background over complete fields.
A special case is stated in~\cite[Remark~16]{M}; the proof here follows the extension and retraction arguments
in the Lean development.
Related Lean formalizations of compatible extension and spherical completion in the nonarchimedean setting
appear in Yuan~\cite[Prop.~6.6 and Thm.~7.3]{Yuan}.

Nonarchimedean norms and spherical completeness are as defined in the introduction.
We write $\overline B(a,r)=\{x:\norm{x-a}\le r\}$, with $r\ge0$.
In a nonarchimedean space, two such balls meet if and only if the distance between their centres is at most the larger radius;
in that case the ball of smaller radius is contained in the other.

Recall from Section~\ref{sec:preliminaries} that $\Mult^k(X;Y)$ and $\Alt^k(X;Y)$ carry the operator norms determined by
\[
\norm{m(x_1,\dots,x_k)}\le\norm m\prod_{i=1}^k\norm{x_i}.
\]
When the target norm is nonarchimedean, both operator spaces are nonarchimedean, regardless of the norm on $X$.

Throughout this subsection, $K$ is a nonarchimedean nontrivially normed field and $k\ge0$ is an integer.
Positive characteristic implies the nonarchimedean hypothesis by Lemma~\ref{lem:ultra}.
We prove the following operator statement, which also records every smoothness order. For normed
$K$\nobreakdash-spaces $E,E'$ and a nonarchimedean, spherically complete $F$,
\[
A^k_{E,E';F}\text{ is }C^n\qquad(n\in\N\cup\{\infty,\omega\}).
\]
Together with spherical completeness of $\Alt^k(E;F)$ and~\eqref{eq:joint-action}, this proves Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}).

No completeness assumption on $K,E$ or $E'$ is needed, and no nonarchimedean hypothesis is imposed on $E$ or $E'$.
For $k\ge1$, put $W=\Alt^k(E;F)$ and $M=\Mult^k(E;F)$. The desired projection is a bounded linear map
$\rho\colon M\to W$ of norm at most one that fixes every alternating map. Once it is available, the formula
\[
P(f_1,\dots,f_k)(m)=\rho\bigl(m\circ(f_1,\dots,f_k)\bigr)
\]
is bounded and multilinear in the operators $f_i$, and has diagonal $A$. This is the required analytic lift:
expansion around any operator gives a finite power series. The construction of $\rho$, outlined after Proposition~\ref{prop:alt-spherical} and carried out in Appendix~\ref{app:spherical}, rests on the spherical completeness of $W$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:alt-spherical}
For a nonarchimedean nontrivially normed field $K$, a normed $K$\nobreakdash-space $E$ and a nonarchimedean, spherically complete $F$, the space $\Alt^k(E;F)$ is a nonarchimedean, spherically complete normed space
for every $k\ge0$.
\end{proposition}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:spherical}.

Proposition~\ref{prop:alt-spherical} is the codomain assertion of Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}).
Appendix~\ref{app:spherical} first proves an extension lemma for spherically complete targets that keeps a
prospective extension in several operator balls at once (Lemma~\ref{lem:compatible-extension}).
It then deduces Proposition~\ref{prop:alt-spherical} by identifying $\Alt^k(E;F)$ isometrically with the bounded
linear maps into $F$ on a free seminormed space that vanish on the multilinearity and equal-input relations.
A second application of the lemma, now with target $W$, extends the inverse of the isometric inclusion $W\to M$
to a contraction $\rho\colon M\to W$ that fixes every alternating map; inserted into the lift formula above, it gives
a bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear lift of $A$ of norm at most one, which completes the proof of
Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}).

\subsection{Consequences for alternating-map bundles}\label{ssec:analytic-bundles}
Each of the three mechanisms gives analytic alternating-map bundles over an arbitrary analytic base, provided the
fibers satisfy the corresponding hypothesis.

\begin{corollary}[analytic alternating-map bundles]\label{cor:analytic-bundles}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field, let $k\ge0$, and let $M$ be an analytic manifold over $K$ modeled on an
arbitrary normed space. Let $\mathcal E,\mathcal H\to M$ be analytic normed vector bundles with typical fibers $E,F$.
Then $\Alt^k(\mathcal E;\mathcal H)$, with fiber $\Alt^k(\mathcal E_x;\mathcal H_x)$ over $x$, is an analytic normed
vector bundle, functorially in analytic bundle morphisms between bundles satisfying the same hypothesis, in each of
the following cases:
\begin{enumerate}
\renewcommand{\theenumi}{\alph{enumi}}
\renewcommand{\labelenumi}{\textup{(\theenumi)}}
\item $k!\ne0$ in $K$;
\item $K$ is nonarchimedean, and $F$ is nonarchimedean and spherically complete, or merely admits an equivalent
norm with these properties;
\item $E$ has a finite algebraic basis with continuous coordinate functionals; for instance, $K$ is complete and
$E$ is finite-dimensional.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For typical source fibers $E_1,E_2$ and a typical target fiber $F_1$ of bundles satisfying the hypothesis, the map
$A^k_{E_1,E_2;F_1}$ is analytic: in case~(a) by Proposition~\ref{prop:factorial-positive}, and in case~(c) by
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-coordinate} and Lemma~\ref{lem:findim}. In case~(b) we may assume $k!=0$ in $K$, so
that $K$ has positive characteristic. Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}) then applies after an equivalent change of
norm on $F_1$, which alters the norms of the alternating-map and operator spaces only by fixed factors and so
preserves analyticity. By~\eqref{eq:joint-action}, $\Alt^k$ is analytic on the relevant hom spaces, and
its composites with the transition maps, the changes of trivialization and the local expressions of bundle morphisms are analytic. Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction}(2) therefore applies (part~(1) is not available in cases~(b) and~(c), where $\Alt^k$ need not be analytic on all hom spaces).
\end{proof}

Cases~(a) and~(b) concern the target fibers alone. Call a normed space $F$ a \emph{universal target} if
$A^k_{E,D;F}$ is analytic for all normed spaces $E,D$, and write $\mathsf T_k(K)$ for the class of universal
targets (formally Definition~\ref{dom:definition} in Section~\ref{sec:domains}). Cases~(a) and~(b) say that
$\mathsf T_k(K)$ contains every normed space when $k!\ne0$ in $K$, and every space with an equivalent
nonarchimedean, spherically complete norm. Their proof uses only this membership, so the corollary holds verbatim
whenever the typical target fibers lie in $\mathsf T_k(K)$. When $k!=0$ in $K$, the analytic conclusion over a general base needs some
hypothesis on the fibers. Section~\ref{sec:families-bundles} removes it for bases with finite continuous
coordinates, and Section~\ref{sec:nonanalytic-bundles} shows that it cannot be removed over general Banach bases.

\section{Analytic families and bundles over finite-coordinate bases}\label{sec:families-bundles}\label{fam:sec:families}

Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction}(2) requires analyticity of the alternating functor only along the
transition families of the input bundles, not on whole hom spaces. This section shows that the weaker requirement
holds for every analytic family of morphisms parametrized by an open subset of $K^d$, in every characteristic and
every degree and with arbitrary normed fibers (Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:finite-families}). The alternating-map bundle
of analytic input bundles is therefore analytic over every base with finite continuous coordinates
(Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-bundles}), which proves Main Theorem~(\ref{main:families}).
For infinite-dimensional parameter spaces we prove the same conclusion under hypotheses on the final target fiber,
the codomain of the value-space component of a family. Over a nonarchimedean field, families parametrized by open
subsets of $c_0(I,K)$ are covered when this fiber is complete and nonarchimedean (Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:c0-families}).
Over any $K$, families parametrized by open subsets of $\ell^1(I,K)$ with the sum norm are covered when this fiber is
complete (Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:l1-families}). The longer proofs for these two classes are in
Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

Regularity on an entire operator space is stronger than regularity along a prescribed family of operators.
This distinction gives a useful replacement for restricting the objects of the alternating-map bifunctor.
We retain every object and every individual morphism, and ask which analytic families the bifunctor preserves.
The resulting family structure is canonical. Its usefulness comes from the concrete parameter classes
for which the additional condition is automatic.

\subsection{The canonical family structure}
For objects $X=(E,F)$ and $Y=(E',F')$ of
$\mathsf{Vec}_K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Vec}_K$, write
\[
 V_X=\Alt^k(E;F),\qquad
 H_{XY}=\cL(E',E)\times\cL(F,F'),
\]
and denote the action on morphisms by
\[
 A_{XY}\colon H_{XY}\longrightarrow\cL(V_X,V_Y),\qquad
 A_{XY}(u,v)m=\Alt^k(u,v)(m)=v\circ m\circ(u,\ldots,u).
\]
The product defining $H_{XY}$ has the maximum norm. These conventions make the contravariance in $E$
explicit: the first component of a morphism $X\to Y$ is a map $E'\to E$.

\begin{definition}\label{fam:def:admissible}
Let $U$ be open in a normed $K$\nobreakdash-space. A map $\gamma\colon U\to H_{XY}$ is
\emph{admissible} if both $\gamma$ and $A_{XY}\circ\gamma$ are analytic.
Families on analytic manifolds are admissible when their expressions in parameter charts are admissible.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}\label{fam:prop:category}
Admissible families contain constants and are closed under analytic reparameterization, local gluing,
and categorical composition. Composition is allowed to depend on either a common parameter or independent parameters.
They form the largest collection of ordinary analytic families for which the action of $\Alt^k$ is analytic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Constants, reparameterization, and gluing follow from the corresponding properties of analytic maps.
For composable families $\gamma\colon U\to H_{XY}$ and $\eta\colon U\to H_{YZ}$, composition in
$H$ is bounded bilinear, and functoriality gives
\[
 A_{XZ}(\eta\circ\gamma)
 =A_{YZ}(\eta)\circ A_{XY}(\gamma).
\]
Composition of bounded operators is bounded bilinear as well, so both required maps are analytic.
For independent parameter spaces, pull the two families back to their product and apply the same argument.
Finally, any ordinary analytic family preserved analytically by $\Alt^k$ satisfies exactly the two
conditions of Definition~\ref{fam:def:admissible}, and hence belongs to this collection.
\end{proof}

Equivalently, identify the set $H_{XY}$ with the graph
\[
 \Gamma_{A_{XY}}=
 \{(h,A_{XY}(h)):h\in H_{XY}\}
 \subseteq H_{XY}\times\cL(V_X,V_Y)
\]
and declare a family analytic when its map to the ambient product is analytic.
No assertion that this graph is an analytic submanifold is involved. In the language of plots, this is a category
whose hom sets are analytic plot spaces: the parameter domains are open subsets of normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces,
the reparameterizations are analytic maps, and covers are open covers. The smooth concrete-sheaf framework
of Baez and Hoffnung~\cite{BH} motivates this formulation; the analytic family structure used here is
specified directly by Definition~\ref{fam:def:admissible} and Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:category}.

This structure need not preserve the vector-space operations on hom sets. Suppose $k>0$ and
$u\colon U\to\cL(E',E)$ is analytic but its pullback action is not. With $F'=F$, both
$(u,0)$ and $(0,\id_F)$ are admissible because their induced actions vanish. Their sum
$(u,\id_F)$ is not admissible. Thus addition of admissible families can fail.
Multiplication by an analytic scalar family does preserve admissibility, since
$A_{XY}(\lambda u,\lambda v)=\lambda^{k+1}A_{XY}(u,v)$.

\subsection{Reflection through a closed subspace}
The range condition behind admissibility can be separated from the operator construction.
Set
\[
 W=\cL(V_X,\Alt^k(E';F')),\qquad
 Z=\cL(V_X,\Mult^k(E';F')),
\]
and let $j\colon W\hookrightarrow Z$ be the map induced by forgetting alternation.
It is a linear isometry with closed image. Indeed, an element of $Z$ belongs to its image precisely when
every value vanishes on every tuple with two equal entries. These are closed linear evaluation conditions;
the operator norms before and after forgetting alternation agree. This argument does not require completeness.

Although $A_{XY}$ can fail to be analytic, its composite with $j$ is always a bounded homogeneous polynomial
of degree $k+1$. A multilinear representative is obtained by using $k$ independent input operators
and one independent output operator:
\begin{equation}\label{fam:eq:ambient-polynomial}
 B\bigl((u_1,v_1),\ldots,(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})\bigr)(m)(x_1,\ldots,x_k)
 =v_{k+1}\bigl(m(u_1x_1,\ldots,u_kx_k)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Its norm is at most one, and its diagonal is $jA_{XY}$. For pullback alone, the corresponding degree is $k$.
Thus an analytic family always gives an analytic ambient map $jA_{XY}\gamma$.
The remaining question is whether this map is analytic into $W$.

We first record how closedness detects homogeneous coefficients. The conclusion concerns their diagonals,
not the values of a chosen multilinear representative on arbitrary tuples.

\begin{lemma}[closedness of homogeneous terms]\label{fam:lem:diagonal}
Let $W$ be a closed linear subspace of a normed space $Z$, and suppose
\[
 f(x_0+h)=\sum_{n\ge0}p_n(h,\ldots,h)
\]
is an analytic expansion with values in $Z$. If $f$ takes values in $W$ near $x_0$, then
$p_n(x,\ldots,x)\in W$ for every $n$ and every parameter vector $x$.
\end{lemma}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

\begin{theorem}[finite-coordinate analytic reflection]\label{fam:thm:finite-reflection}
Let $j\colon W\hookrightarrow Z$ be a linear isometry with closed image, let $U\subseteq K^d$ be open,
and let $f\colon U\to W$. If $jf$ is analytic, then $f$ is analytic.
The spaces $W,Z$ and the field $K$ need not be complete. The same statement holds when the parameter space
is continuously linearly isomorphic to $K^d$.
\end{theorem}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

\begin{remark}[Preservation of the coefficient bound]\label{fam:rem:radius}
In the proof of Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection} (\S\ref{app:ssec:finite-reflection}), the Taylor coefficients $p_n$ of $jf$ are regrouped into monomial coefficients $c_\alpha\in W$ and $W$\nobreakdash-valued multilinear maps $q_n$ with the same diagonals. If $Z$ has a nonarchimedean norm, both finite sums in that construction satisfy maximum estimates.
Thus $\norm{c_\alpha}\le\norm{p_n}$ and $\norm{q_n}\le\norm{p_n}$ for maximum-norm
coordinates on $K^d$. The reflected expansion has at least the radius of the ambient expansion,
without completeness of $W$ or $Z$. The $c_0$ theorem below extends this same estimate to infinitely
many coordinates; there completeness supplies the infinite coefficient sums.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}\label{fam:cor:finite-families}
Every analytic morphism family parametrized by an open subset of $K^d$ is admissible for $\Alt^k$,
in every characteristic and every degree, with arbitrary normed fibers.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection} to the closed isometric inclusion $j$ above and the analytic map
$jA_{XY}\gamma$, using~\eqref{fam:eq:ambient-polynomial}.
\end{proof}

Over an incomplete field, finite algebraic dimension alone does not imply the existence of continuous
coordinates. The coordinate hypothesis in Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection} is consequently essential
to its stated scope. When $K$ is complete, every finite-dimensional normed parameter space has such coordinates.
In particular, standard finite-dimensional analytic probes cannot detect the failure of analyticity of
the full operator action. Arbitrary normed parameter spaces, including the operator spaces themselves,
are needed to recover the regularity notion used in the Main Theorem.

\subsection{Bundles over finite-coordinate bases}\label{fam:sec:bundles}
The finite-coordinate theorem gives a bundle construction in every characteristic, even when the fibers
are infinite-dimensional. For certain infinite-dimensional bases, summability gives further positive results.
These are conditions on the parameter space of a family, and should be distinguished from the conditions
on source or target fibers in the earlier positive theorems.

Here an analytic normed vector bundle has operator-valued analytic transition maps in its local
trivializations. Bundle morphisms are required to have operator-valued analytic expressions as well.
For bundles $\mathcal E,\mathcal H$ over the same analytic manifold $M$, with typical fibers $E,F$, take a common
trivializing cover with transition maps $g_{ij}$ and $h_{ij}$. As in \S\ref{ssec:bundle-construction}, the candidate
transition maps on alternating forms are
\begin{equation}\label{fam:eq:bundle-transitions}
 \theta_{ij}(x)\colon m\longmapsto h_{ij}(x)\circ m\circ
 \bigl(g_{ji}(x),\ldots,g_{ji}(x)\bigr),\qquad m\in\Alt^k(E;F).
\end{equation}
The inverse $g_{ji}=g_{ij}^{-1}$ is an analytic operator family by the bundle atlas.
Functoriality gives the cocycle identity for~\eqref{fam:eq:bundle-transitions}.

\begin{theorem}[alternating-map bundles]\label{fam:thm:finite-bundles}
Let $M$ be an analytic $K$\nobreakdash-manifold modeled on $K^d$, or on a normed space with continuous
linear coordinates in $K^d$. For every $k\ge0$, fiberwise alternating maps define a bifunctor
\[
 \Alt^k\colon
 \mathsf{VB}^{\omega}_K(M)^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{VB}^{\omega}_K(M)
 \longrightarrow\mathsf{VB}^{\omega}_K(M),
\]
where $\mathsf{VB}^{\omega}_K(M)$ denotes analytic normed vector bundles over $M$ with the
operator-valued analytic bundle morphisms just specified.
There is no restriction on the characteristic, the degree, or the dimensions and completeness of the fibers.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
In each base chart, the pair
$(g_{ji},h_{ij})$ is an analytic family with a finite-coordinate parameter space.
Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:finite-families} makes~\eqref{fam:eq:bundle-transitions} analytic.
The cocycle identity therefore glues the local products with fiber $\Alt^k(E;F)$ to an analytic normed
vector bundle. The same argument applies to a change of trivialization and to the local expressions
of a pair of bundle morphisms, with the first morphism directed contravariantly. The identities and
composition laws hold fiberwise by functoriality of alternating maps, giving the stated bifunctor.
\end{proof}

Together with the unconditional $C^\infty$ result, this supplies the alternating-map bundle construction
for every smoothness order over such finite-coordinate bases. The analytic conclusion does not assert
that the action on arbitrary operator spaces is analytic; it uses the base charts as parameter spaces.
Over a complete field, the hypothesis includes every finite-dimensional normed model space.

Finite dimension enters the proof only through Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection}: the ambient
transition $j\circ\theta_{ij}$ of~\eqref{fam:eq:bundle-transitions} is analytic over every base, by~\eqref{fam:eq:ambient-polynomial},
and the finitely many monomial coefficients give $W$\nobreakdash-valued representatives with the exponential
bound~\eqref{fam:eq:finite-bound}. No reflection step is needed, over any base, when the joint action itself has a
bounded multilinear lift: when $k!\ne0$ in $K$, when the target fibers lie in $\mathsf T_k(K)$, or when the source fibers have
finite continuous coordinates. The hypothesis on the base cannot simply be removed: Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:banach-base-failure} in
Section~\ref{sec:nonanalytic-bundles} gives analytic bundles over a Banach base whose alternating-map bundle is not
analytic.

\subsection{Nonarchimedean \texorpdfstring{$c_0$}{c0} parameters}
For an arbitrary index set $I$, write $c_0(I,K)$ for the space of scalar families tending to zero outside
finite sets, with its supremum norm. Completeness is imposed on the output of the coefficient construction,
not on the field or the parameter space.

\begin{theorem}\label{fam:thm:c0-reflection}
Let $K$ be nonarchimedean, let $j\colon W\hookrightarrow Z$ be a linear isometry, and assume that
$W$ is complete and $Z$ has a nonarchimedean norm. If $U\subseteq c_0(I,K)$ is open and
$jf\colon U\to Z$ is analytic for a map $f\colon U\to W$, then $f$ is analytic.
\end{theorem}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

\begin{corollary}\label{fam:cor:c0-families}
Over a nonarchimedean field, every analytic morphism family parametrized by an open subset of
$c_0(I,K)$ is admissible for $\Alt^k$ when its final value-space target $F'$ is complete and nonarchimedean.
The same holds after a bounded linear isomorphism of parameter spaces.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
When $F'$ is complete and nonarchimedean, so are $\Alt^k(E';F')$ and the operator space $W$;
the ambient operator space $Z$ is nonarchimedean as well.
Apply Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:c0-reflection} to $A_{XY}\gamma$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ordinary \texorpdfstring{$\ell^1$}{l1} parameters}
For this subsection, $\ell^1(I,K)$ has the ordinary sum norm
$\norm{x}_1=\sum_{i\in I}\abs{x_i}$, even when $K$ is nonarchimedean.
The proof of Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:l1-families} below uses the fixed degree of the outer polynomial. It does not require a uniform lifting bound
for all Taylor degrees of an arbitrary ambient analytic map.

\begin{theorem}[fixed-degree reflection along \texorpdfstring{$\ell^1$}{l1} families]
\label{fam:thm:l1-families}
Let $W$ be a closed subspace of a Banach space $Z$, let $H$ be normed, and suppose that
$P\colon H\to W$ is the diagonal, in $Z$, of a bounded $d$\nobreakdash-linear map $B\colon H^d\to Z$.
For every analytic map $\gamma\colon U\to H$, where $U$ is open in $\ell^1(I,K)$,
the map $P\gamma\colon U\to W$ is analytic. The field $K$ need not be complete.
\end{theorem}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

\begin{corollary}\label{fam:cor:l1-families}
Every analytic morphism family parametrized by an open subset of $\ell^1(I,K)$ is admissible for
$\Alt^k$ when the final value-space target $F'$ is complete. The same holds when the parameter space
is a bounded linear retract of an $\ell^1(I,K)$ space. No completeness is required of the other fibers.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The ambient space $Z=\cL(V_X,\Mult^k(E';F'))$ is Banach, and $W$ is closed in $Z$.
Apply Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:l1-families} to the degree-$(k+1)$ polynomial~\eqref{fam:eq:ambient-polynomial}.
For pullback alone one may instead use degree $k$.
If $i\colon P\to\ell^1(I,K)$ and $r\colon\ell^1(I,K)\to P$ are bounded linear with $ri=\id_P$,
apply the result to $\gamma r$ on the open set $r^{-1}(U)$, and then restrict along $i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Failure of descent through quotient parameters]
\label{fam:cor:quotient-parameters}
Let $K$ be a complete nontrivially normed field of characteristic $p>0$,
and let $k\ge p$. There are Banach spaces $E,F$, an index set $J$, and a
bounded open linear surjection
\[
 q\colon\ell^1(J,K)\longrightarrow H:=\cL(E,E)
\]
such that $A^k_{E,E;F}\circ q$ is analytic, whereas $A^k_{E,E;F}$ is
analytic at no point. The map $q$ has no local right inverse on a nonempty open subset of $H$ that is
Fr\'echet differentiable at even one point of that subset.
\end{corollary}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:families}.

Thus an analytic family can become admissible after a bounded linear
quotient reparameterization even though it was not admissible originally.
The open-cover locality of admissible families does not imply descent
through such quotient maps. In particular, the bad parameter space $H$
cannot be a bounded linear retract of an ordinary $\ell^1$ space.

The bundle argument of Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:finite-bundles} now gives two further forms of the construction:
over bases modeled on $c_0(I,K)$ when the output fibers are complete and nonarchimedean, and over bases
modeled on $\ell^1(I,K)$ or its bounded linear retracts when the output fibers are complete.
For a statement about an entire category of bundles, one can restrict all fibers respectively to
nonarchimedean Banach spaces or to Banach spaces; alternating-map fibers remain in the same category.

On arbitrary analytic bases one can instead specify admissible transition families and require admissibility
of changes of atlas and bundle morphisms. Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:category} ensures compatibility with
composition. When the alternating-map spaces at both endpoints are Banach (in particular, when the
value fibers are complete), an admissible family of isomorphisms with analytic underlying inverse family
has an admissible inverse family. Its alternating action is the inverse of the original action. Locally
identify the two endpoint spaces by one fixed isomorphism; inversion is then analytic by the convergent
Neumann series in the Banach endomorphism algebra.
The shear example of Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:banach-base-failure} shows why arbitrary analytic changes of atlas cannot simply be substituted for
admissible ones in this formulation.

\subsection{Universal analytic reflection}
The parameter theorems (Theorems~\ref{fam:thm:finite-reflection}, \ref{fam:thm:c0-reflection}
and~\ref{fam:thm:l1-families}) suggest an intrinsic question: when does every analytic map into a Banach
space remain analytic into each closed subspace containing its values?
Appendix~\ref{app:families} first isolates the exact local coefficient problem for a general parameter
space (Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:coefficient-criterion}). Over a complete field, \S\ref{fam:sec:tensors}
then characterizes this universal reflection property of a Banach space $P$
(Definition~\ref{fam:def:universal-reflection}) by projections $R_n$ from the completed projective tensor
powers $T_n(P)$ onto their diagonal subspaces $\Delta_n(P)$, the closed spans of the pure powers
$x^{\otimes n}$, with exponentially bounded norms (Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:tensor-analytic}).

For $K^d$ with the maximum norm, the coordinate construction gives $\norm{R_n}\le d^n$. For the ordinary
sum-norm space $\ell^1(I,K)$ over a complete nonarchimedean field, with $I$ infinite, the infimum of the
norms of such projections is exactly $n!$ (Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:l1-optimal}). So $\ell^1(I,K)$ lacks
universal analytic reflection, although every analytic morphism family parametrized by an open subset of
it is admissible for $\Alt^k$ when its final value-space target is complete
(Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:l1-families}). The alternating-family property is strictly weaker because the
ambient alternating action has one fixed polynomial degree, so factoring through $\ell^1$ costs a single
factorial bound rather than one for each Taylor degree. Over a general Banach base no such reflection
need be available: Section~\ref{sec:nonanalytic-bundles} gives, in characteristic $p>0$ and degree $k\ge p$,
analytic bundles over a Banach base whose alternating-map bundle, with the induced transitions,
is not analytic.

\section{Nonanalytic functors give nonanalytic bundles}\label{sec:nonanalytic-bundles}

The analytic results of Sections~\ref{sec:functor-bundles} and~\ref{sec:families-bundles} each use an extra
hypothesis, such as $k!\ne0$ in $K$, nonarchimedean spherically complete target fibers, source fibers with finite continuous
coordinates, a base with finite continuous coordinates, or a $c_0$ or $\ell^1$ base with complete target fibers (nonarchimedean in the $c_0$ case). This section carries out the second step of the program
described in the introduction. When $k!=0$ in $K$ and no such hypothesis is imposed, analyticity fails both for the
alternating functor and for the alternating-map bundles it induces.

When $k!=0$ in $K$, there are Banach spaces $E$ and $F$ for which the pullback action $A^k_{E,E;F}$ is analytic at no point
(Theorem~\ref{thm:main}). Hence $A$ is analytic for all Banach spaces exactly when $k!\ne0$ in $K$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:class}). The shear of Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:shear} turns a point of nonanalyticity of $A$ into a
point of nonanalyticity of the pullback along an affine analytic family of automorphisms. Applied to
Theorem~\ref{thm:main}, it gives an alternating-map bundle over a Banach base whose transition between the charts
induced by two analytic trivializations is analytic at no point (Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:banach-base-failure}). This
proves Main Theorem~(\ref{main:failure}).

Two refinements concern the base. In \S\ref{lau:ssec:multipliers}, over $K=\F_q((u))$, the operator base is replaced
by the open unit ball of $c_0(\N,K)$ and the shear by diagonal isometric transitions. Among Banach model spaces
admitting an equivalent nonarchimedean norm over this field, finite dimension is then exactly the right hypothesis
on the base (Corollary~\ref{lau:cor:dichotomy}). Over the incomplete field $\F_p(t)$, the alternating-map bundle of
analytic input bundles can also fail to be analytic over a base of finite algebraic dimension without continuous
coordinates. This uses the objects of Section~\ref{sec:domains} and is recorded there as
Remark~\ref{dom:finite-algebraic-base}.

\subsection{A nowhere analytic alternating functor}\label{ssec:counterexample}
As noted in the introduction, the usual polarization argument divides by $k!$ and does not apply when that
scalar vanishes. The following theorem shows that this
obstruction is unavoidable if analyticity is required for all Banach spaces over $K$.

\begin{theorem}[Counterexample theorem]\label{thm:main}
Let $K$ be a nontrivially normed field of characteristic $p>0$, and let $k\ge p$. There are Banach spaces $E$ and $F$
over $K$ such that the degree-$k$ map
\[
A\colon\cL(E,E)\to\cL\bigl(\Alt^k(E;F),\Alt^k(E;F)\bigr),\qquad A(f)(m)=m\circ(f,\dots,f),
\]
is analytic at no point of $\cL(E,E)$. Equivalently, there is no bounded $k$\nobreakdash-linear map $P$ on $\cL(E,E)$ with
$P(f,\dots,f)=A(f)$ for all $f$. The Banach space $F$ admits no equivalent nonarchimedean norm.
The same $E,F$ work for any reindexing of the $k$ arguments.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:class}
Let $K$ be any nontrivially normed field and $k\ge0$ any integer. Then $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is analytic for all
$K$\nobreakdash-Banach spaces $E,E',F$ if and only if $k!\ne0$ in $K$. More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}
\item if $k!\neq0$ in $K$, then $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is analytic, indeed given at every point by a finite power
series, for all normed spaces $E,E',F$, complete or not;
\item if $k!=0$ in $K$, then there are Banach spaces $E=E'$ and $F$, with $F$ admitting no equivalent
nonarchimedean norm, such that $A^k_{E,E;F}$ is analytic at no point.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $k!\ne0$ in $K$, Proposition~\ref{prop:factorial-positive} gives a finite power series at every
point for arbitrary normed $E,E',F$, including degree zero. If $k!=0$ in $K$, the field has positive prime
characteristic $p\le k$. Theorem~\ref{thm:main} gives the required Banach counterexample. Together these prove the
classification and both strengthened conclusions.
\end{proof}

\needspace{8\baselineskip}

Thus the factorial condition is exact, even when the source and target of the linear maps coincide.
The failure is also local in the strongest sense: in the examples, analyticity fails at every base point.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Main Theorem~(\ref{main:general})]
The map $(v,T)\mapsto v_*\circ T$ in~\eqref{eq:joint-action} is bounded bilinear, and every $A^k_{E,E';F}$ is
$C^\infty$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-positive}. Hence every joint action, and thus the bifunctor, is $C^\infty$.
Suppose $k!\ne0$ in $K$. In degree zero, $A$ is constant and the joint action is a bounded linear function of
$v$ alone. For $k\ge1$, Corollary~\ref{cor:class} and Proposition~\ref{prop:lift} give a bounded
$k$\nobreakdash-linear lift $P$ of $A$. Placing the pushforward variable in the last slot,
\[
\bigl((u_1,v_1),\dots,(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})\bigr)\longmapsto(v_{k+1})_*\circ P(u_1,\dots,u_k)
\]
is a bounded $(k+1)$\nobreakdash-linear lift of the joint action. Expanding it as in Proposition~\ref{prop:lift}(1)
gives a finite power series at every point. Suppose $k!=0$ in $K$, and let $E=E'$ and $F$ be the Banach spaces of
Corollary~\ref{cor:class}(2). By~\eqref{eq:joint-action}, composing the joint action with the affine map
$u\mapsto(u,\id_F)$ recovers $A^k_{E,E;F}$. Hence the joint action is analytic at no point of the form $(u,\id_F)$.
For the restriction to $\mathsf{Ban}_K$, recall from the introduction that $\Alt^k(E;F)$ is complete whenever $F$ is complete. The restricted bifunctor has the same maps on hom spaces, and the counterexample spaces are Banach,
so both assertions persist.
\end{proof}

It remains to prove Theorem~\ref{thm:main}. The shear construction below uses only the statement of the theorem,
so it comes first. The Banach spaces behind the theorem and an outline of its proof follow in
\S\ref{ssec:laurent-counterexample}, and a detailed roadmap of the proof opens Appendix~\ref{app:FF}.
\subsection{The shear construction}
Restricting to changes of frame does not remove the obstruction on unrestricted parameter spaces.
The following elementary construction transfers any bad pullback map to a family of automorphisms.

\begin{proposition}[invertible shear family]\label{fam:prop:shear}
Let $E,D,F$ be normed $K$\nobreakdash-spaces, and suppose
\[
 A\colon\cL(E,D)\longrightarrow
 \cL\bigl(\Alt^k(D;F),\Alt^k(E;F)\bigr)
\]
is not analytic at $u_0$. Put $H=D\oplus E$, with the maximum norm, and define
\[
 g(u)=\begin{pmatrix}\id_D&u\\0&\id_E\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Then $g$ and $g^{-1}$ are affine analytic operator families, but
$u\mapsto g(u)^*$ on $\Alt^k(H;F)$ is not analytic at $u_0$.
In particular, the counterexample can occur at $u_0=0$, where $g(0)=\id_H$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The inverse is $g(-u)$. Let
\[
 J\colon\Alt^k(D;F)\to\Alt^k(H;F),\qquad Jm=m\circ\pi_D^{\times k},
\]
and let $R\colon\Alt^k(H;F)\to\Alt^k(E;F)$ be restriction along $i_E\colon E\to H$.
Both maps are bounded linear, and $\pi_Dg(u)i_E=u$, so
