The composite $A^k_{E,E;F}\circ q$ is analytic everywhere, while $A^k_{E,E;F}$ is nowhere analytic.
Every local section of $q$ on an open subset fails to be Fr\'echet differentiable at each point
of its domain, and this same $H$ is not a bounded linear retract of $\ell^1(I,K)$ for any index set $I$.
The norms on $H$ and $F$ need not be nonarchimedean.

Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:tensor-homogeneous} and Theorem~\ref{fam:thm:tensor-analytic}
are also formalized over a complete nontrivially normed field $K$ and a Banach parameter space $P$.
The tensor powers are separated completions for the ordinary sum projective seminorm,
with the norm-controlled universal property for Banach targets.
The homogeneous lift preserves the diagonal and has norm at most $\norm B\norm{R_n}$.
The analytic criterion characterizes reflection into every closed subspace of every Banach target,
on every open subset of $P$, by one family of projections $R_n\colon T_n(P)\to\Delta_n(P)$,
$n\ge1$, with a common bound $\norm{R_n}\le A^n$ for some $A\ge1$.
The equivalent finite root-limsup condition is included, and the degree-zero Taylor coefficient
is handled separately. The necessity proof uses the actual dependent $c_0$ Banach test space
and its ambient-analytic pure-power family on the whole open unit ball.

The checked development further includes the joint action and its functorial laws, the core
admissible-family laws and graph characterization of Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:category},
Proposition~\ref{fam:prop:shear} with its Banach counterexample at the identity, and the
canonical-support identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:canonical-support}. The degree-zero and
degree-one support cases and finite-ambient contraction-matrix formula are included.
These proofs have passed Lean verification without admitted proofs. Statement-review results
and declaration-level scope are recorded in the repository theorem map.

The open-manifold family theorem and analytic bundle instance in Main Theorem~(\ref{main:families})
are formalized. The declaration
\texttt{contMDiffVectorBundle\_\allowbreak{}alternating\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}finiteCoordinates}
uses Mathlib's existing alternating-bundle topology, for arbitrary $K$, degree and normed model fibers,
and supplied continuous coordinates $P\simeq K^d$, without completeness assumptions.
The declaration \texttt{alternatingBundleFunctor} completes Main Theorem~(\ref{main:families})
as an actual bifunctor on analytic normable bundles. Its object and morphism formulas use the existing
bundle topologies and operator-valued analytic Hom sections; identity, composition and transport
between analytically compatible presentations are formalized.
Corollary~\ref{fam:cor:banach-base-failure}, which is Main Theorem~(\ref{main:failure}), is formalized as
\texttt{exists\_\allowbreak{}banach\_\allowbreak{}base\_\allowbreak{}alternatingBundle\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}analytic},
over every nontrivially normed field of characteristic $p$ and every $k\ge p$. The shear bundle is a Mathlib
vector bundle core over the Banach base $\cL(E,E)$ with two global charts; it and the trivial bundle with fiber $F$
are analytic, but the actual alternating-map bundle, with Mathlib's existing topology, is not analytic. The
multiplier strengthening of \S\ref{lau:ssec:multipliers}, the dichotomy of Corollary~\ref{lau:cor:dichotomy} and
Remark~\ref{dom:finite-algebraic-base} are not formalized.
Main Theorem~(\ref{main:domains}) is also formalized over the $t$\nobreakdash-adic field $K=\F_p(t)$,
for every prime $p$ and every $k\ge p$. The checked statement rules out a largest full analytic domain,
also with isomorphism closure and within the finite-dimensional nonarchimedean ambient category
(with isomorphism closure relative to that ambient category). It includes the incompatible analytic
objects, the split-destination obstruction and the analytic completed models of Remark~\ref{dom:limits}.
The witnesses are incomplete; this result does not settle the corresponding questions over complete
fields or among Banach pairs. The general bundle construction of Proposition~\ref{prop:bundle-construction} and its consequences over general bases (Corollaries~\ref{cor:smooth-bundles}, \ref{cor:analytic-bundles} and~\ref{cor:domain-bundles}), the $c_0$ and $\ell^1$ bundle constructions, the remaining tensor results,
the remaining analytic-domain results and finite-support matrix transport remain pending.
The theorem map records their scope and status.

\paragraph{Scalar targets and ambient forms.}
Main Theorem~(\ref{main:scalar}), Theorems~\ref{thm:scalar-counter}, \ref{thm:scalar-tangent}
and~\ref{thm:scalar-pullback}, Proposition~\ref{prop:universal-zero-dual}, and the countable-type scalar
case of Proposition~\ref{prop:scalar-countable} are new mathematical results, not new Lean theorems in this
repository. In particular, the new finite fibre obstruction, chain-space construction and their assembly
are not yet formalized. The finite-dimensional target equivalence uses the informal reduction above.

The arbitrary-arity tail input from the characteristic-$2$ development~\cite{M} was independently rebuilt,
together with its eight-module local dependency closure, using Lean~\texttt{4.34.0-rc2}.
The declarations \texttt{AltCounter.tail\_multilinear} and
\texttt{AltCounter.nsc\_of\_hasEmptyNestedBalls} use only
\texttt{propext}, \texttt{Classical.choice} and \texttt{Quot.sound}; their statements have no
characteristic-two restriction. The latter takes an explicit empty nested sequence of balls as its
premise; the elementary reduction from conventional nonspherical completeness is proved mathematically
in Appendix~\ref{app:scalar}.

Four auxiliary vector-space lemmas for forms were also independently compiled against the existing
Mathlib branch at \texttt{6d235fec7752}, with the same standard axioms: ambient analytic pullback,
reflection from ambient analytic regularity to intrinsic $C^\infty$ regularity, symmetry of the second
derivative, and $d^2=0$. These checks use the branch's compiled dependencies and are not a clean rebuild
of Mathlib or a claim about upstream master. They do not formalize the manifold-level form sheaf,
the shuffle wedge, the preservation of ambient analyticity by exterior differentiation, or the scalar
bundle and pullback counterexamples. The corresponding mathematical statements of
Main Theorem~(\ref{main:forms}) are proved in \S\ref{sec:scalar-forms}.

The proposed alternative route of Remark~\ref{rem:routeII}, the proposed large-index construction of
Remark~\ref{rem:R}, the open problems, and Serre's external classification cited in
Remark~\ref{rem:sorted} are excluded from the proved results. The repository's comparator challenge contains
two short contradiction corollaries of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}, the operator form of Main Theorem~(\ref{main:spherical}) for every regularity order,
the finite-coordinate analytic alternating-bundle theorem, a contradiction from the analytic alternating-bundle
instance with no restriction on the field, model with corners, base manifold, normed fibers or finite index type,
and the nonexistence of a largest full analytic domain over $\F_p(t)$ in degrees $k\ge p$.
Their solutions use the corresponding library theorems. The Banach and spherical-completeness hypotheses
are given by transparent definitions in Mathlib terms. The largest-domain predicate uses the development's
actual categorical definition of joint analyticity on every full hom space. Intentional placeholders occur only in the challenge
file, not in the proof library or solution. Current challenge checks use Kim Morrison's comparator, with the
configuration recorded in the repository.
